✻ Brewed for 5m 7s · done 11:02

❯ explain what the pilot go/no-go is?                                           

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

● The pilot go/no-go is phase 8 of the T43 plan in PLAN.md, line 178. It was a
  checkpoint to stop and review a small sample before solving the whole game.

  - Scope: the first four submaps, 0x00–0x03. The LLM agent, driving the solver
    through MCP, produces the input scripts for those submaps, and you watch
    them play.
  - What to record: solver time and number of LLM calls per submap, to estimate
    the cost of doing all 47 submaps.
  - The decision: you judge whether the play looks acceptable (natural,
    efficient, no deaths), then say "go" to scale up to phase 9, the full run,
    or "no-go" to rework the approach first.

  The gate is now effectively past: we went ahead with phase 9 without a formal
  answer, and the whole game is solved. What's left of it is:

  1. Your visual verdict on the play quality, now on the whole game rather than
     0x00–0x03. That fits naturally into phase 10, where you watch the full demo
     in the SDL build.
  2. The cost figures (solver time and LLM calls per submap). They were never
     recorded systematically. Solver times are only in the individual solve
     results, and LLM calls weren't counted.

  So you could close phase 8 as "superseded by the full run" and fold the visual
  verdict into phase 10. Or, if you still want a sign-off on the sample, I can
  export just 0x00–0x03 for you to watch first.

  in a temporary worktree of master; say if you'd rather have it built somewhere
  permanent.

  What changed on master
  - Castle loop fix (98dbaca): the demo used to hold one script per submap. Map
    3's castle is entered twice (0x15/0x16), so the export kept only the second
    pass, and the demo died on the first one. It now holds one script per submap
    visit (49 in all). A death still replays the current visit's script.
  - End-of-game loop (same commit): when the demo finishes the game, it fades
    back to the title screens and starts a new game, with no game over screen
    and no hall of fame. Normal play and recording end exactly as before.
  - The demo itself (c9c35ed): the solved run replaces the old 4-submap script.
    The -demo help text now says that -map/-submap only match a script recorded
    from the same start, and that the demo loops. Both commits are merged into
    solver.

  Checks

    headless replay over the whole game (25283 lines). It then looped back to
    0x00.

    from the previous game; it doesn't affect play.

    solver timeline still validates 154/154.

● Found the key issue: since emscripten 5.0.4, EMSCRIPTEN is only defined inside
  emscripten.h, but the port's #ifdef EMSCRIPTEN guards it before including
  that header, so with a current emcc all guards silently fail and the browser
  hangs on the native loop. Now checking GAME_PERIOD and other EMSCRIPTEN-keyed
  code in master.

● I wrote wasm.md at the repo root, next to PLAN.md, and changed nothing else
  (the file isn't committed). I didn't try a build: the only emsdk on this
  machine is the 2019 one, and installing a current one would change your
  system.

  How it was done (2019): a bash script, build/emsdk/build.sh, built the game
  with emscripten 1.38.30, still installed at D:\d\EmSdk. It compiled each
  source file with SDL2 and bundled a data/ folder into the output. A player.js
  page showed a "click to play" overlay, passed the command-line options, and
  added touch controls. On the C side, EMSCRIPTEN guards switch to a
  browser-driven main loop and skip the blocking calls.

  What has changed since:
  - The sources are now split into src/rd1/ and src/rd2/, but the script still
    only compiles the top-level src/ folder.
  - The port moved from SDL2 to SDL3.
  - The data files and zlib are gone; everything is compiled in.
  - The sound engine is now C++.
  - The build now needs -fcommon and -DPLATFORM_ST.
  - The -data option was removed, but player.js still passes it, so the game
    would exit immediately.

  Tools have moved on a lot:
  - Emscripten is at 6.0.10, released a week ago. It has an SDL3 port
    (-sUSE_SDL=3) and needs Python 3.10 or newer.
  - Module.callMain must now be exported explicitly.
  - The most important change: since emscripten 5.0.4, the plain EMSCRIPTEN
    macro is only defined inside emscripten.h, and the port includes that header
    only inside an #ifdef EMSCRIPTEN. With a current compiler, all the web code
    paths would silently disappear and the browser tab would hang.
  - SDL3 reads the code field of keyboard events, while player.js sends only
    keyCode, so the touch pad and on-page buttons would do nothing.

  Would master build for the web today? No. Four things block it:
  1. The build script is stale.
  2. -data makes the game exit at startup.
  3. The macro change above hangs the tab.
  4. The RD2 engine uses blocking loops, so it would need ASYNCIFY or a rewrite.
     A web build of RD1 alone is the realistic first step.

  There's also a timing bug dating from 2019: the web loop runs at 24 frames per
  second, while native runs one frame every 75 ms (about 13 per second). So the
  web version played about 1.8× too fast, and the screens that change speed at
  runtime had no effect.

  The document ends with the list of changes an update would need. It marks what
  I verified and what I didn't; one point, for example, is recalled rather than
  checked against emscripten's changelog.

          -__EMSCRIPTEN__` … should be                                   
       79 -  used instead)", plus a warning on its use. **This breaks the
          - port silently**: every guard

          -en.h` *inside* such a guard →

       85 -  (`-sEXPORTED_RUNTIME_METHODS=callMain`, default empty); "don
          -'t run main" is the

          -ntion `Module.noInitialRun`

          -omp bitcode; with upstream the                                
       89 -  normal form is `emcc -c f.c -o f.o` (**unverified** how curr
          -ent emcc treats `-o *.bc`                                     
       90 -  without `-c`). Or simply compile and link in one `emcc`/`em+
          -+` call, or `emmake make`.                                    
       91 -- **Browser-side SDL3 behaviour** (vendored SDL3 3.4.16 source
          -/docs):

          -cripten_HandleKey`:

          - `player.js`'s synthetic

          -ch pad and buttons dead;                                      
       95 -  - audio still needs a user gesture before sound plays (the c
          -lick overlay already does it);

          -ementations), so the NULL

          -x unconditionally, fine

          -rame` (fps 0) main loops.

      131 -## 5. What an update would involve (not done)                 
      142 +RD2 is a transcription of the 68000 flow. It never returns to 
          +a frame loop:                                                 
      143 +- **Blocking loops.** `for (;;)` state loops in `rd2_flow.c` (
          +lines 32, 181, 325, 434,                                      
      144 +  508); busy waits such as `while (!FIRE())`, `while (FIRE())`
          + and                                                          
      145 +  `while (rd2_snd_rw(0x1aa08) != 0)`; the pause loops in `rd2_
          +game.c:107-108`.                                              
      146 +- **Frame wait.** `rd2_191e6` (VBL) spins on `rd2_sys_pump()`,
          + which polls events, runs                                     
      147 +  the joystick and **`sys_sleep`s** until the next 50 Hz tick 
          +(`rd2_sys.c:282-297`).                                        
      148 +- **Exits.** `rd2_sys_pump` calls `exit(0)` on window close or
          + ESC.

      150 +**Options:**                                                  
      151 +- **A. ASYNCIFY (recommended).** Link with `-sASYNCIFY` and ma
          +ke the sleep in                                               
      152 +  `rd2_sys_pump` an `emscripten_sleep(ms)`, which hands contro
          +l back to the browser.                                        
      153 +  Every loop then yields through the one place where the engin
          +e already waits for the                                       
      154 +  VBL. Restrict the instrumentation to the call paths that rea
          +ch `emscripten_sleep`                                         
      155 +  (`-sASYNCIFY_ONLY=…` or `ASYNCIFY_ADD`/`REMOVE` lists) to li
          +mit code size and slow-down;                                  
      156 +  size it after a first build. The busy waits that do not go t
          +hrough `rd2_sys_pump` need                                    
      157 +  checking one by one: each must reach `rd2_191e6`/`rd2_sys_pu
          +mp` or it spins forever.                                      
      158 +  RD1 can keep `emscripten_set_main_loop`; ASYNCIFY does not a
          +ffect it.                                                     
      159 +- **B. Restructure as a per-frame state machine.** Faithful bu
          +t very large; it would                                        
      160 +  rewrite the transcription the KB validates line by line. Not
          + recommended.                                                 
      161 +- **C. Run RD2 on a pthread (web worker)** with rendering prox
          +ied to the main thread.                                       
      162 +  Needs COOP/COEP headers and a threads build (a build with th
          +reads cannot also run                                         
      163 +  without them); rendering must stay on the main thread. Not r
          +ecommended.                                                   
      164 +                                                              
      165 +**Other RD2 points:**                                         
      166 +- `rd2_snd.c` creates its mutex unconditionally, which is harm
          +less without threads                                          
      167 +  (**unverified** at run time).                               
      168 +- The debug hooks (`RD2_INPUT`, `RD2_SHOT`, `RD2_TRACE`, `RD2_
          +JOYSEQ`, …) read                                              
      169 +  environment variables and write files. Under emscripten `get
          +env` sees only what                                           
      170 +  `Module.ENV` sets, and files go to MEMFS. That is fine; the 
          +hooks are simply unused on                                    
      171 +  the web.                                                    
      172 +- `exit(0)` in the pump ends the runtime. The page should show
          + "game over / reload"                                         
      173 +  instead of a frozen canvas.                                 
      174 +- RD2 audio has the same user-gesture constraint as RD1.      
      175 +- In phase W1 the RD2 sources stay compiled in, since `xrick.c
          +` references                                                  
      176 +  `rd2_game_run`, but `-rd 2` is refused on the web with a cle
          +ar message.

● emscripten 6.0.10 works. The plan is in wasm.md, and nothing else in the repo
  changed (the file isn't committed).

  Toolchain check
  - A plain source emsdk_env.sh fails in Git Bash: emsdk calls python, which
    here is the Windows Store alias. It works once you point it at the bundled
    Python first (and don't source it through a pipe):
  cd /d/d/EmSdk
  export EMSDK_PYTHON="$PWD/python/3.13.3_64bit/python.exe"
  source ./emsdk_env.sh
  - The SDL3 port (-sUSE_SDL=3, SDL 3.4.2) builds on first use, and a test
    program runs under node.
  - The test confirmed the risk from the analysis: the old EMSCRIPTEN macro is
    only defined after including emscripten.h, with a deprecation warning.
  - master as it is: all 67 C files and 8 C++ files compile, and they link into
    xrick.js + xrick.wasm (2.1 MB). But the compiled objects contain the native
    paths (the blocking main loop and SDL_WaitEvent), so the page would hang.
    With -DEMSCRIPTEN, the old web paths compile against SDL3.
  - Not done: running the build in a browser.

  Plan, phase W1 (RD1 only), one documented commit per step on master:
  1. A new build script that mirrors the Makefile (SDL3, C++ audio engine, no
     data files).
  2. Switch the guards to __EMSCRIPTEN__.
  3. Fix the timing: a browser-paced loop that runs a logic step each time a
     game period of real time has passed. The web build currently runs 1.8× too
     fast and ignores runtime speed changes.
  4. Refuse -rd 2 on the web.
  5. A new minimal page: no jQuery, no -data, and key events that carry code, so
     the touch controls work.
  6. Measure the audio engine's cost in the browser.
  7. Check determinism: the demo trace from the web build should match the
     native one line for line, then you check it visually.
  8. Packaging, and updating the build docs.

  RD2 (documented for phase W2): its engine never hands control back to the
  browser; all its waits go through one frame-wait function that sleeps. The
  recommended fix is to link with -sASYNCIFY and make that sleep an
  emscripten_sleep, which yields to the browser. Every busy loop then needs
  auditing to make sure it passes through that wait. The document also covers
  the two alternatives I don't recommend (a full restructure, or running RD2 on
  a worker thread), and the smaller points (audio mutex, debug hooks, exit(0)).

  Questions for you, also listed in wasm.md:
  1. This emsdk is Windows-native, so it runs from Git Bash, while the project
     default is to build in WSL. Keep Git Bash, or install a Linux emsdk in WSL?
  2. Where should the web page live: in this repo under build/emsdk/, or in a
     separate deployment repo?
  3. Should the site start in demo mode?

● Found the issue: with the default EXIT_RUNTIME=0, exit(1) never reaches the
  runtime, so onExit isn't triggered. I'll fix this by adding -sEXIT_RUNTIME=1
  and exporting _fflush in build.sh, and updating game.c to call
  emscripten_force_exit(0) on quit so the runtime actually exits and flushes
  stdio.

  Checks, in headless Chrome:

    lines: the whole game plus the start of the second loop.

    play it, so whether it sounds right still needs your ears.
  - Native builds are unchanged (ST 230, PC 231 warnings); the web build has 168
    clang warnings.

  Decision needed: game speed. The native loop has a timer quirk: it measures
  the frame interval after the previous sleep, so it alternates a full sleep
  with none. It actually runs about 25.7 steps per second, twice the nominal
  13.3. The web loop now runs at exactly the nominal rate, so it plays half as
  fast as native (12.6 measured). The 2019 web build's fixed 24 fps was probably
  chosen to match native. Options:
  - A. Match the web to native's actual pace. This is what I recommend.
  - B. Keep the nominal rate and fix the native timer on master, which would
    make the whole game play half as fast as today.
  - C. Measure the original ST game's rate in Hatari first, then set both.

  per second, one step per 40 ms: the main loop waits for 2 vertical blanks

  The measurement. I ran the original ST game (the same build our Ghidra dump
  comes from) and counted VBLs between consecutive main-loop iterations:
  - With Rick standing still for 20 s, 606 of 608 iterations took exactly 2
    VBLs.
  - With Rick walking right for 15 s, 425 iterations took 2 VBLs. The few longer
    ones were room redraws and the respawn after a death, which do much more
    work than a normal frame.
  - This matches what the disassembly predicts.

  Current speeds compared with the ST's 40 ms per step:

  ┌────────────┬─────────────────────────┬────────────┐
  │   build    │  ms per gameplay step   │ vs the ST  │
  ├────────────┼─────────────────────────┼────────────┤
  │ native     │ about 37.5, and jittery │ 7% fast    │
  ├────────────┼─────────────────────────┼────────────┤
  │ web (W1.3) │ 75                      │ half speed │
  └────────────┴─────────────────────────┴────────────┘

  The native build is jittery because of a timer bug: frames alternate between a
  full sleep and none. That bug also affects the other screens (scrolling,
  intro, game over): each runs at about half its nominal period today.

  Proposal for setting both (in wasm.md, not done yet):
  1. On master, one documented commit:
     - fix the native timer so each frame lasts exactly its period;
     - set GAME_PERIOD to 40, the ST rate;
     - halve the scrolling, intro and game-over periods so those screens keep
       today's pace (they weren't measured on the ST).

     it gets the right speed once master is merged into it.

          +ue; when a frame                                              
      266 +   * runs late, the schedule restarts from now instead of catc
          +hing up                                                       
      267 +   * (wasm.md §8, kb/hatari.md 2026-09-28).                   
      268 +   */                                                         
      269 +  tmx = sys_gettime() - tm;  /* time since this frame was due 
          +*/                                                            
      270 +  if (tmx < game_period)                                      
      271 +  {                                                           
      272 +    sys_sleep((int)(game_period - tmx));                      
      273 +    tm += game_period;                                        
      274 +  }                                                           
      275 +  else                                                        
      276 +    tm = sys_gettime();                                       
      277  #endif

● The master timing fix is committed and the docs are updated, but I couldn't
  rebuild the web version: the permission check has stopped answering for the
  build command, so the web re-measurement is also still to do.

  Master fix (89d0e1a):
  - The native loop now holds one frame per period. The old code timed each
    frame from before its sleep, so frames alternated between a full sleep and
    none and ran at about half the period, jittery.
  - Gameplay period is 40 ms on ST, the rate I measured on the original in
    Hatari. PC gets 38 ms, which keeps the pace it had (PC wasn't measured).
  - Scroll, title and game-over periods are halved (to 12, 25 and 25 ms) so
    those screens keep today's pace; I didn't measure them on the ST.

  Native checks:
  - The demo at default speed now runs 23.9 logic steps per second, in line with
    the 24.0 measured on the ST while walking (scroll frames and room redraws
    don't count as logic steps).
  - The fast demo trace is byte-identical to the previous one over 37013 lines,
    so gameplay is unchanged.
  - Builds are clean; warnings went down by one each (ST 229, PC 230) because
    the old timer line warned.

  wasm branch: master is merged in (05e43f3). The web loop already scheduled one
  step per period, so it needs no code change and now runs at the ST rate. I
  updated its comment (806fc8f).

  Docs (outer repo 4c1455e): wasm.md §8, kb/demo.md, and notes in
  kb/xrick/architecture.md, divergences.md, algo-level.md, algo-system.md and
  MEMORY.md. The kb/xrick pages describe the original 2019 xrick tree, so they
  got "since 89d0e1a" notes rather than rewrites. The Hatari measurement is in
  kb/hatari.md (committed earlier).

● Bash(git -C /d/d/reverse/xrick log --oneline -1)  ⎿  504b2f9 Web build: ./build-wasm.sh at the top of the port repo (regist

● The build script is now ./build-wasm.sh at the top of the port repo (next to
  build/ and demo.sh), and it works from a plain Git Bash. I named it
  build-wasm.sh rather than build.sh so it isn't mistaken for the native build;
  the page files stay in build/emsdk/.

  Why it failed before: the script only loaded the emsdk environment when emcc
  wasn't already found, hid the output of that step, and ran it with "stop on
  first error" already on. When emsdk's setup script returned an error, the
  build stopped with no message. A failed compile also stopped it silently.

  What it does now:
  - It always loads the emsdk environment first. It uses emsdk's bundled Python
    and shows emsdk's own output if emcc still can't be found. EMSDK_DIR
    overrides the default /d/d/EmSdk.
  - A compile error prints the error lines instead of stopping silently.
  - The file keeps LF line endings and is executable in git.

  Checks:
  - From a fresh bash with no emsdk on the PATH, ./build-wasm.sh clean gz found
    emscripten 6.0.10, compiled 75 files (168 warnings) and linked.
  - When emsdk's setup fails (I tested with a stripped environment), the script
    now prints its error message.
  - The web rebuild ran at 23.6 logic steps per second in headless Chrome,
    against 23.9 for native.
  - The fast demo trace is byte-identical to native over 55026 lines, more than
    two full demo loops.

  Committed on wasm as 16fc448; the docs (kb/build.md §4, wasm.md §8) are in the
  outer repo as 504b2f9. I also updated my build note to point at the new
  script.

  Still open from before: solver hasn't been merged with master, and the wasm
  checkout still lives in my scratchpad (…/scratchpad/wt-wasm). To run the page
  from there: ./build-wasm.sh, then serve build.web/.

  repo:
  1. Checks first, before building anything. It stops with a clear message if it
     can't find:
     - MSBuild: found through vswhere, or set MSBUILD to override;
     - SDL3 for the desktop build, in xrick/vcpkg_installed/x64-windows; the
       message points to the one-time vcpkg setup;
     - emsdk: EMSDK_DIR (default /d/d/EmSdk) must exist and contain emsdk_env.sh
       and upstream/emscripten/emcc. It then loads the emsdk environment itself
       and shows emsdk's own output if emcc still can't be found.
  2. Desktop: Release x64 through MSBuild, output xrick/bin/Release/xrick.exe.
  3. Web: same as before, output build.web/.
  4. Arguments: clean rebuilds both from scratch; gz adds gzip copies of the web
     files.

    and the web build with 168 warnings.

    stopped the script at once with their message.

● ./build.sh now puts the Windows version in build/win and the web version in
  build/web. Committed on master as 964807d; docs updated in the outer repo
  (9327903).

  What changed in build.sh:
  - Windows: MSBuild is told to put the executable and objects in build/win/
    (objects under build/win/obj/). The project's post-build step copies
    SDL3.dll next to the executable, and the script now fails if that DLL isn't
    there. clean empties build/win first.
  - Web: output goes to build/web/, with the gzip copies in build/web/gz/.
  - .gitignore: now ignores /build/web/ and /build/win/ instead of build.web/.
  - New check: the two web page files (build/emsdk/index.html and player.js) are
    now checked up front with the other requirements. Before, a missing page
    file was skipped silently and build/web ended up with no page.

  Checks, from a fresh bash: ./build.sh clean gz put xrick.exe and SDL3.dll in
  build/win, with the usual 3 MSVC warnings. The web build compiled and linked
  into build/web. It didn't finish its last step because the page files were
  missing (see below), so the full web output in build/web isn't checked yet.

  Needs you: build/emsdk has been renamed to build/emsdk.nope in your checkout
  (at 16:09, not by me). Git sees that as the two page files being deleted, so
  ./build.sh now stops at once with "web page source missing:
  …/build/emsdk/index.html". I left the rename in place in case it was a
  deliberate test of the checks. To restore it, run mv build/emsdk.nope 
  build/emsdk (or git checkout -- build/emsdk) from the top of the port repo,
  then run ./build.sh again for a full build.

  - .gitignore now relies on /build/ alone; __pycache__/ stays ignored.

    rebuilt in the new place.

  Checks:

  - With the new paths, the whole-game chain (s422) replays 154 of 154 legs
    correctly under the merged code.

  Docs: kb/demo-solver.md (§3 and §12) and PLAN.md are updated, in outer repo
  commit c06426a.

  Things to know:
  1. The main checkout is on solver now, because the MCP server runs from it;
     you had it on master. Run git checkout master there if you want master
     back, but then the MCP tools won't work.
  2. The MCP server that's currently running still has the old paths loaded.
     Reconnect it with /mcp before using the xrick tools again.
  3. Some build output still sits next to the sources (all git-ignored):
     - the native WSL Makefile build: *.o files under src/ and the xrick binary
       in xrick/;
     - xrick/bin/ and xrick/obj/ from earlier direct MSBuild runs.

     The Makefile is master's, so moving its output under build/ too would be a
     change on master. Say if you want it.

  as f9048ff), and the leftover MSBuild folders are gone.

  Makefile (master):
  - The binary is build/wsl/xrick, objects go in build/wsl/obj/, and make warn
    writes its log to build/wsl/warn.log.
  - make clean removes build/wsl.
  - ST and PC builds share that folder, as before, so run make clean when
    switching PLATFORM.

  - xrick/bin/ and xrick/obj/ from earlier direct MSBuild runs, including a
    run3.log from an old run in bin/Release;
  - the old .o files next to the sources, and the old xrick/xrick binary.

  where vcpkg installs SDL3 for the Windows build.

  now removes build/wsl and build/core, and leaves the MCP states in build/mcp
  alone.

    the 9733 trace lines I compared.

    chain still replays 154 of 154 legs.
